<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;</TD>

<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom">%&nbsp;</TD></TR>
</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In US$ million, unless noted otherwise. EBITDA = Operating income + Depreciation of PP&amp;E + Depreciation of the right of
use assets + Amortization of intangible assets + Unproductive exploratory drillings + (Reversal) / Deterioration of PP&amp;E. Adjusted EBITDA = EBITDA that excludes IFRS 16 effects +/- <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">one-off</FONT> items. Net
Leverage Ratio = Net Debt / LTM Adj. EBITDA. FCF = Cash flow from Operations less CAPEX (Investing activities), M&amp;A (Investing activities), and interest and leasing payments (Financing activities). Fuels = diesel + gasoline. R&amp;M is refining
and marketing business, it excludes petrochemicals and agro businesses. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adjusted EBITDA</B> totaled US$1,245&nbsp;million (+15% vs. 4Q23), on the back
of better local fuels prices in dollar terms, coupled with lower cost pressure after the devaluation of <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">mid-December.</FONT> These effects were partially offset by decreases in FX regime for exporters and replacement
cost of our inventories. Interannually, the increase was even higher, driven by oil output growth. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Total hydrocarbon production</B> averaged 526
kboe/d (+3% both in q/q and y/y), boosted by shale contribution, representing now 49% of the total. Crude oil remained high at 255 kbbl/d (flat q/q but +7% y/y), on the back of a 21% interannual growth in <B>shale oil</B> that continued delivering
positive results, reaching 112 kbbl/d in 1Q24 (+3% q/q and 21% y/y), while <B>Medanito oil exports to Chile</B> amounted to 23 kbbl/d (+22% q/q). </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Domestic fuel&#146;s demand </B>declined by 11% q/q, mainly due to retail demand contraction and seasonal diesel demand, resulting in lower fuels imports <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-80%</FONT> q/q and <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-66%</FONT> y/y), representing only 4% of local fuels sales in 1Q 24. <B>Processing levels at our refineries</B> averaged 301 kbbl/d, reaching a refining utilization
rate of 92% (+4% q/q). </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX </B>totaled US$1,252&nbsp;million, decreasing 15% q/q and 4% y/y, mainly affected by devaluation of <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">mid-December.</FONT> More than 50% of the total was concentrated in shale operations, in line with the Company&#146;s strategy for the short-term growth. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Free cash flow</B> totaled a negative US$394&nbsp;million, compared to a negative US$60&nbsp;million in 4Q23, considering that the payment of imports
deferred from 2023 and debt services were not fully compensated by the positive cash flow from operations, raising <B>net debt</B> to US$7.2&nbsp;billion, while maintaining <B>net leverage ratio</B> at 1.7x. </P>
<P STYLE="line-height:8.0pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:2pt;border-bottom:1px solid #000000;width:11%">&nbsp;</P>

<TD WIDTH="4%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">1</SUP>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman; " ALIGN="left">1 ADR = 1 share. Total issued capital stock amounted to 393,312,793 shares as of
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Mar-2024</FONT> (51% Argentina Government; 36% NYSE and 13% ByMA). </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net Revenues</B> amounted to US$4,310&nbsp;million (+3% q/q), mainly driven by higher local fuels prices in dollar terms
and higher Medanito crude oil exports to Chile, partially offset by lower local demand of fuels, particularly diesel, and fertilizers. Moreover, during 4Q23, our subsidiaries with functional currency in AR$<SUP
STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">2</SUP> were affected by the devaluation of <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">mid-December.</FONT> </P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

<TD>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD>&nbsp;</TD></TR>
</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Stock variations include price effects by (US$29) million in 1Q23, (US$29) million for 4Q23 and (US$169) million for 1Q24.
</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>OPEX</B> totaled US$1,380&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-6%</FONT> q/q), mostly explained by the sharp devaluation of <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">mid-December,</FONT> and to a lesser extent, cost-efficiencies gained in the downstream and upstream segments, such as tariff reduction on supply chain. These effects were partially offset by the impact of devaluation on
our subsidiaries with AR$ as functional currency during 4Q23. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Total Purchases and Stock Variations</B> amounted to US$1,088&nbsp;million <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-16%</FONT> q/q), primarily due to lower imports of fuels due to demand contraction in 1Q24, while 4Q23 was also affected by programmed stoppage at La Plata refinery. These effects were partially offset by restoration of
biodiesel&#146;s availability, higher crude oil purchases, in line with local oil price recovery, and a negative stock variation, even more than 4Q23, as a result of lower replacement cost of our inventories. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net other operating results</B> represented a US$6&nbsp;million gain (vs. US$152&nbsp;million in 4Q23), mainly due to lower FX regime for exporters after
devaluation. Finally, a <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">non-recurring</FONT> <B>impairment </B>charge of US$1,782&nbsp;million was recorded in 4Q23, as a result of the revision of recoverable value of conventional mature fields<SUP
STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">3</SUP> in the upstream. Also, this effect reduced <B>depreciation and amortization</B> in 1Q24 to US$652&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-16%</FONT> q/q). </P>
<P STYLE="line-height:8.0pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:2pt;border-bottom:1px solid #000000;width:11%">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="4%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">2</SUP>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman; " ALIGN="left">The financial information of our subsidiaries, having AR$ as functional currency, is adjusted for inflation,
corresponding to a hyperinflationary economy in accordance with IAS guidelines, and consolidated using the exchange rate for the end of the period. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net financial results</B> represented a US$259&nbsp;million loss (vs. a US$549&nbsp;million gain in 4Q23), mainly due to
the extraordinary impact of the devaluation on net monetary-liability position in AR$ during 4Q23, and to a lesser extent, lower gains from holding financial securities in 1Q24. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">The <B>income tax</B> was positive at US$121&nbsp;million in 1Q24 (vs. a charge of US$860&nbsp;million in 4Q23), mainly due to lower deferred tax liabilities,
as inflation outpaced devaluation in 1Q24. Consequently, <B>net result</B> returned to positive territory at US$657&nbsp;million (vs. a US$1,861&nbsp;million loss in 4Q23). </P>

<TD>&nbsp;</TD></TR>
</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled almost US$2.0&nbsp;billion in 1Q24 (+14% q/q), mainly driven by recovery in crude oil prices (+15%),
coupled with higher crude oil volume sold (+2%), as a result of a better pricing landscape in the local market, while the contribution from natural gas remained almost unchanged. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Lifting cost</B> reached US$12.9/BOE <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-16%</FONT> q/q), primarily due to lower cost pressures in local currency, as a
result of the sharp devaluation occurred by <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">year-end,</FONT> as well as increased shale production and aforementioned cost-efficiencies. Our <B>conventional </B>blocks averaged US$21.5/BOE <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-16%</FONT> q/q), while <B>unconventional </B>activities averaged US$4.6/BOE <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-8%</FONT> q/q). Zooming in, lifting cost of our <B>shale core hub</B> blocks, on a gross basis (at 100%
stake), also declined to US$3.4/BOE <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-15%</FONT> q/q), far below the already competitive levels of around US$4/BOE recorded during 2023. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Royalties </B>and other taxes averaged US$6.7/BOE (+11% q/q), mostly as a result of higher crude oil realization price. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adjusted EBITDA</B> totaled US$839&nbsp;million (+27% q/q), driven by higher crude oil prices and lower OPEX due to devaluation, partially offset by
reduced FX regime for exporters. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> amounted to US$1.0&nbsp;billion <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-7%</FONT> q/q but similar to 1Q23),
where 65% was allocated to drilling and workover activities, 30% to new facilities or the expansion of existing ones, and 5% to exploration and other upstream activities. 73% of total capex was deployed within the unconventional areas. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Drilling and workover activities</B> showed a positive trend, completing a total of 67 new wells in our operated blocks: 29 horizontal shale (all of them
oil) and 38 conventional. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 6/14 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>
<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">


<IMG SRC="g832513logo.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In terms of <B>efficiencies within our shale operations</B>, during 1Q24 the Company continued setting new
records on drilling and fracking performance, averaging 290 meters per day in drilling and 219 stages per set per month on fracking, increasing by 9% and 11%, respectively vs. the previous quarter. Moreover, during February, we achieved the highest
drilling speed for one well in Aguada del Cha&ntilde;ar block, reaching 475 meters per day for a well of almost 4,000 meters of horizontal length, which was fully drilled in 15 days. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Regarding our <B>exploratory strategy</B>, a few days ago we started drilling the first offshore ultra deepwater well at
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">CAN-100</FONT> block, located 315 km from the port of Mar del Plata in the Province of Buenos Aires. Moreover, a few days ago we finished drilling the first horizontal well at El Cerrito Block, in the Palermo Aike
formation, which is the second largest unconventional resource in Argentina after Vaca Muerta. </P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

<TD></TD>
<TD></TD>
<TD></TD>

<TD VALIGN="bottom" WIDTH="1%"></TD>
<TD></TD>
<TD></TD>
<TD></TD></TR>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil production</B> in 1Q24 continued at very high levels, averaging 255 kbbl/d, on the back of shale growth (+3%
q/q), offsetting the drop in conventional output. Additionally, 9% of conventional output came from tertiary production, growing by 34% y/y and minimizing the natural decline in mature fields. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Natural gas production</B> increased by 6% q/q, mainly because 4Q23 was affected by a contraction in demand.<B> NGL production</B> also grew by 6% q/q,
normalizing after the weather events that affected the clients of our subsidiary Mega in the City of Bah&iacute;a Blanca. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>4.2 DOWNSTREAM </B></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">As of 4Q23, the Company has decided to merge the Industrialization and Commercialization segments into a single Downstream segment. In addition, sales of
natural gas were reallocated to the Gas and Power segment, restating comparative information corresponding to the period from 1Q22 to 3Q23. This reclassification is aligned with the new management structure and aims at simplifying the business
reporting and maximizing synergies. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 7/14 </P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Stock variations include price effects by (US$73) million in 1Q23, US$37&nbsp;million for 4Q23 and US$102&nbsp;million for
1Q24. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled US$3,782&nbsp;million in 1Q24 <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-4%</FONT> q/q), mainly due to a local demand contraction
of fuels, particularly diesel, and fertilizers, partially offset by higher local fuels prices and the growing oil exports to Chile. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Refining and
logistics costs </B>decreased by 18% q/q, primarily driven by the sharp devaluation that occurred in <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">mid-December</FONT> 2023, as well as lower transportation costs due to decreased volumes sold and efficiencies
gained, such as tariff reduction. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Fuel imports</B> declined by 83% q/q, mostly because 4Q23 was affected by record high local fuels demand and
programmed stoppage at La Plata refinery, coupled with demand contraction and lower international price in 1Q24. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil purchases</B> (including
intersegment) increased by 18% q/q, mostly on the back of higher local price, besides a slight expansion in volume (higher processing level). <B>Biofuel purchases</B> grew 58% q/q, particularly because 4Q23 was affected by local supply constraints
of biodiesel, while bioethanol was similar to 4Q23. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adjusted EBITDA,</B> excluding inventories price effect of oil products, totaled
US$569&nbsp;million (+6% q/q), mainly due to higher local fuels prices, combined with lower cost pressure after devaluation, partially offset by local crude oil price hike and lower FX regime for exporters. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adjusted EBITDA of the Refining</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; Marketing</B> business, in unit terms, reached US$19.1/bbl in 1Q24, compared to US$16.8/bbl
reported in 1Q23. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> totaled US$206&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-36%</FONT> q/q): 69% refining, 15% midstream oil and 16%
logistics. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In our <B>refineries,</B> during 1Q24 CAPEX was allocated in the following main projects: </P>
<P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B></B>New fuel specifications project including the construction of a new diesel oil hydrotreatment unit at
Luj&aacute;n de Cuyo refinery and a gasoline hydrotreatment plant and revamping of existing gasoline units at La Plata refinery, expected to be operational by 2Q24 and 4Q25, respectively. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>
<P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

Chica-Puesto Hern&aacute;ndez pipeline, which is expected to be completed by 2Q24 and will increase the evacuation of crude oil from our core hub blocks to the northern of the Neuqu&eacute;n Province, to be either exported to Chile through the
Trans-Andean Oil Pipeline or <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">re-directed</FONT> to our Lujan de Cuyo refinery. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

projects aimed at unlocking the evacuation capacity of the Neuquina basin: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">

total evacuation capacity of 345 Kbbl/d by 2025. In addition, <B>OTE</B> continued working the construction of two new storage facilities of 50 Km3 each and the revamping of the export terminal at Puerto Rosales. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

together with the <B>Vaca Muerta North</B> pipeline, operational since last November, allowed us to export 23 kbbl/d of Medanito oil to Chile in 1Q24 (+22% q/q), expecting further increase in the coming months. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Net Average domestic prices for gasoline and diesel are net of taxes, commissions, commercial bonuses and freights. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil processed </B>stood at 301 Kbbl/d in 1Q24 (+4% q/q), as 4Q23 was impacted by maintenance stoppages at La Plata refinery. However, interannually,
it decreased by 2% as in 1Q24 La Plata refinery was also affected by unavailability of OTE&#146;s terminal to deliver crude oil, in addition to heavy rains and flooding in nearby areas. This effect was partially offset by better performance at
Luj&aacute;n de Cuyo refinery, which during last March reached the highest monthly processing mark in its history. Consequently, the Company recorded a refinery utilization of 92% in 1Q24. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Domestic fuels sales volumes </B>decreased by 11% q/q, driven by a decrease of 14% and 7% in diesel and gasoline demand, respectively, on the back of
retail demand contraction and lower seasonal diesel volumes. Interannually, local fuels sales dropped by 2%, especially on the back of a 4% decline in diesel demand, affected by lower industrial and agro business activity. Nonetheless, the Company
gained local fuels market share to almost 60%, as Argentina&#146;s total fuels market contraction was even further <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-6%).</FONT> </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Petrochemicals volumes</B> grew by 21% q/q, primarily because 4Q23 was affected by a lower methanol demand for the biofuels market. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 9/14 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>
<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">


<IMG SRC="g832513logo.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Fertilizers, grain and flours sales volumes</B> declined by 20% q/q, mainly due to a contraction in local
demand of fertilizers. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net average fuels prices in the domestic market measured in dollar terms </B>increased by 11% y/y, as a result of an active
pricing policy that has been deployed by the Company since the end of last year, through which it has been possible to reduce the gap vs. import parity to 7% in 1Q24, compared to 20% in 4Q23. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Prices for petrochemical</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; other refined products</B> declined by 8% q/q, aligned with a downward trend in international prices of
certain refined products such as jet fuel, asphalts and lubricants. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>4.3 GAS&nbsp;&amp; POWER </B></P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled US$482&nbsp;million in 1Q24, increasing by 52% q/q, mainly because in 4Q23 our subsidiary Metrogas,
with functional currency in AR$, was affected by the sharp devaluation of <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">mid-December,</FONT> partially offset by lower seasonal LNG regasification revenues in 1Q24. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Natural gas purchases</B> grew by 19% q/q, mostly in line with higher natural gas sales, while <B>operating costs</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; other</B>
expanded significantly, primarily led by devaluation mentioned above. Moreover, in 1Q24 the Company recognized higher charge for doubtful sales receivables for US$29&nbsp;million, within operating costs, according to Resolution N&deg; 58/24 of the
Secretary of Energy, released on May&nbsp;8, 2024<SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">4</SUP>. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">As a result of the aforementioned factors,
<B>Adjusted EBITDA</B> totaled a negative US$30&nbsp;million, compared to a negative US$ 38&nbsp;million. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> totaled US$12&nbsp;million <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-71%</FONT> q/q) and continued focusing on the following main midstream gas facilities expansion projects, aiming at <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">de-bottlenecking</FONT> the potential of the Vaca Muerta formation:
</P> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B></B>Revamping of NGL facility &#147;Tex Loma La Lata&#148;: first stage became online since September 2023,
and expected to be fully operational by 2Q24, adding a potential processing capacity of up to 6 Mm3/d of natural gas and 600 tons/d of NGL in the core hub blocks. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">FCF = Cash flow from Operations less capex (Investing activities), M&amp;A (Investing activities), and interest and leasing
payments (Financing activities). </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net cash flow from operations </B>reached US$1,089&nbsp;million in 1Q24 <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-36%</FONT>
q/q). Despite the sequential increase in Adjusted EBITDA, it was affected by payment of 2023&#146;s imported goods and services differed to 1Q24 and certain delays in the collections of gas sales. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net cash flow from investing activities</B> was negative US$1,208&nbsp;million in 1Q24, compared to negative US$1,563&nbsp;million in 4Q23, primarily due
to the impact of devaluation in <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">mid-December.</FONT> </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net cash flow from financing activities</B> amounted to positive
US$312&nbsp;million in 1Q24 (vs. negative US$111&nbsp;million in 4Q23), mostly driven by the issuance of the international bond (+US$800 million), partially offset by prepayment of 40% of 2024&#146;s international bond
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$138</FONT> million), in addition to regular debt service. Moreover, the Company continued securing trade facilities. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">As a result, the <B>free cash flow</B> for the period was negative U$394&nbsp;million, as the deployment of our capital expenditure and our regular interest
payments were not fully compensated by the cash flow from operations. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In terms of liquidity, our <B>cash and short-term investments</B> stood at
US$1,599&nbsp;million by the end of March 2024, increasing 15% vs. 4Q23. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>5.2 NET DEBT </B></P>

<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">


<IMG SRC="g832513logo.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">As of March&nbsp;31, 2024, YPF&#146;s <B>consolidated net debt</B> totaled US$7,200&nbsp;million, increasing
by US$397&nbsp;million q/q and US$1,157&nbsp;million y/y. Despite higher net debt compared to the previous quarter, the Company managed to maintain <B>net leverage ratio</B> around 1.7x. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In terms of <B>financing</B>, in January 2023 the Company issued an export-secured bond for a nominal value of US$800&nbsp;million with a fixed rate of 9.50%,
maturing in July 2031 and amortizing in 10 semiannual installments since July 2026. On the other hand, a cash tender offer was launched for the 2024 Notes (US$346 million), which resulted in prepayment of near 40% of the outstanding amount of said
notes. The remaining amount was fully paid at maturity, on April 4<SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">th</SUP>, 2024. Additionally, the Company paid at maturity final amortization of Series XIII Notes (US$43 million) and refinanced trade
financing facilities. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Regarding our <B>maturity profile</B>, the Company faces debt maturities for the remaining 9 months of 2024 for
US$1,169&nbsp;million, mainly concentrated in short-term trade facilities for over US$400&nbsp;million and international bonds&#146; amortizations. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">The
following chart shows our consolidated principal debt maturity profile as of March 2024: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt" ALIGN="center">


<IMG SRC="g832513g0509075227398.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 12/14 </P>

<P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>7. ABOUT YPF </B></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">YPF is the largest energy company in
Argentina, fully integrated in the oil and gas value chain. Our main businesses are: (i)&nbsp;in the upstream, we produce roughly 35% and 30% of the country&#146;s crude oil and natural gas, respectively; (ii)&nbsp;in the downstream, we operate 3
refineries representing approximately 50% of Argentina&#146;s refining capacity and lead the local diesel and gasoline sales with a market share of more than 55%; and (iii)&nbsp;in gas and power, Metrogas, our subsidiary, distributes around 25% of
the country&#146;s natural gas, while YPF Luz, our affiliate, is the third largest power generation company in Argentina. The Government is the controlling shareholder with a 51% stake, and YPF is listed in the NYSE and ByMA. </P>
